And at work—I do sheet metal work—I've been missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. Then I get frustrated and end up snapping at the younger guys on the crew, which isn't like me at all. I feel awful about it, I really try to be a good coworker. \n\nI've even stopped going out on the water, and I haven't been working on my metal sculptures because... I just don't see the point right now. Does that make sense? I really am trying to push through it. Is it... is it bad that the distractions aren't working? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get past this.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to enjoy, like working on your metal sculptures or going out on the water, don’t bring you any pleasure anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I haven't really tried them enough lately to know for sure. I feel bad even saying that, because I know I should be keeping up with my hobbies. It's just... I haven't really had the energy to get the boat ready, or to set up my torch at home. \n\nSo it's not that I don't like doing those things anymore! Do you think I should try to force myself to do them this weekend?\n\nDoctor: When you think about your metal sculptures or going out on the water, do you feel any sense of enjoyment or satisfaction just from thinking about doing them, even if you haven’t actually done them lately?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still like the idea of them, I think. Like, when I picture being out on the water, it sounds nice... in theory. I don't want you to think I've just given up on everything I care about. \n\nIt's just that when I actually think about all the steps it takes to get the boat hitched up, or to haul the metal out to the garage and get everything set up... it just feels like so much work right now. The thought of it just makes me feel really tired instead of excited. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm really not trying to be negative about it. I know I need to get back to my normal life. Does it sound like I'm just being lazy about it? Because I promise I'm usually a really hard worker.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I... I guess if I'm being completely honest, and I feel really terrible admitting this... it kind of feels like they just don't matter right now. I just don't see the point. I'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful and I really don't want you to think I'm just giving up on life.

I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for letting things slip this much at work and at home. Does that make me sound crazy? I just really hope it's normal to feel this off track when things change.

I... gosh, I feel like I'm just giving you the same answer over and over, and I really don't mean to be difficult. I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking.

But even when I try to do something else, it's like this heavy feeling is just... always there in the background. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm not trying hard enough to get better. I really am trying to push through it. Is it... is it bad that the distractions aren't working? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to get past this.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to enjoy, like working on your metal sculptures or going out on the water, don’t bring you any pleasure anymore?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I haven't really tried them enough lately to know for sure. I feel bad even saying that, because I know I should be keeping up with my hobbies. It's just... I haven't really had the energy to get the boat ready, or to set up my torch at home. \n\nSo it's not that I don't like doing those things anymore! I'm sure if I just forced myself to go out there, I'd probably still enjoy it. I just... I haven't been doing them. I hope that's an okay answer... I'm sorry if I'm not being very helpful. Do you think I should try to force myself to do them this weekend?\n\nDoctor: When you think about your metal sculptures or going out on the water, do you feel any sense of enjoyment or satisfaction just from thinking about doing them, even if you haven’t actually done them lately?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still like the idea of them, I think. Like, when I picture being out on the water, it sounds nice... in theory. I don't want you to think I've just given up on everything I care about. \n\nIt's just that when I actually think about all the steps it takes to get the boat hitched up, or to haul the metal out to the garage and get everything set up... it just feels like so much work right now. The thought of it just makes me feel really tired instead of excited. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm really not trying to be negative about it. I know I need to get back to my normal life. Does it sound like I'm just being lazy about it? Because I promise I'm usually a really hard worker.\n\nDoctor: When you think about doing the things you used to enjoy, like your metal sculptures or being on the water, does the thought of them still bring you any pleasure or satisfaction, or does it feel like those things don’t really matter to you anymore?\n\nPatient: I... I guess if I'm being completely honest, and I feel really terrible admitting this... it kind of feels like they just don't matter right now. I just don't see the point. I'm so sorry, I know that sounds awful and I really don't want you to think I'm just giving up on life. \n\nIt's just... ever since my daughter left for basic training, I come home to what feels like a dead house, and I just can't seem to keep myself together. I'm really trying, I promise I am. But I've been sitting in my truck crying before my shifts, which is so embarrassing. And at work... I've been missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years. I've even been snapping at the younger guys on the crew. That is *not* who I am, I swear. I try to be a good coworker. \n\nSo when it comes to the boat or my sculptures... I just don't see the point in them right now when I can barely handle my actual responsibilities. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for letting things slip this much at work and at home. Does that make me sound crazy? I just really hope it's normal to feel this off track when things change.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're a failure or that you're letting people down more than you should be, especially because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really think of myself as a failure, exactly. I know I'm going through a hard time, and I'm trying my best to handle it. I really don't want to let anyone down, especially at work, which is why I feel so bad about snapping at the guys. \n\nBut I don't sit around thinking I'm worthless or anything like that. I know I'm a good mom and a hard worker, usually. I just... I feel like I should be handling this transition better, you know? Like I should be stronger. Is it normal to feel like you're letting yourself down a little bit when you can't just bounce back from something? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about where my head is at.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the things you used to really enjoy—like your metal sculptures or being out on the water—does doing them sound appealing to you now, or does it feel like you wouldn't get any real satisfaction from them even if you tried?\n\nPatient: I... I think I already kind of answered this, but I don't mind explaining it again if I wasn't clear before. I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. \n\nLike I said, it just feels like they don't matter right now. I don't think I would get any real satisfaction out of them if I tried. It's just so hard to care about hobbies when I'm struggling so much with the important things, like my job and just... keeping my head above water since my daughter left. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I'm repeating myself, and I really hope I don't sound like a broken record or like I'm just whining. I'm just trying to be as honest as I can so you can help me. Did that answer the question better this time?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’re not getting pleasure or satisfaction out of most things you do these days, even the things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: I... gosh, I feel like I'm just giving you the same answer over and over, and I really don't mean to be difficult. I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nI guess... yeah, it's pretty much most things right now. Even the things I used to love. It just all feels kind of flat. I'm really sorry, I know that probably sounds so negative, and I promise I'm usually a much more upbeat person. I just can't seem to find the joy in much of anything since she left. Is it okay that I'm feeling that way? Like, does that make sense for what I'm going through?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or have you felt restless or unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've had that. I hope it's okay to say no to that one, I really don't want to mess up your evaluation or anything. But physically, I feel like I'm moving at my normal pace. \n\nI'm still keeping up with all the heavy lifting and the regular pace at the shop, and I haven't felt jittery or like I can't sit still. I'm just... you know, moving normally, maybe just a little tired. I hope that's a good sign? Like, maybe that means I'm not doing too terribly? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, so I hope it's alright that I don't really have that issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or that you're feeling more tired out than you used to, even when you're not doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know I said just a second ago that I was a little tired, but... no, I don't think I have less energy overall. Like, I'm not exhausted when I'm just sitting around at home or anything. \n\nMy job at the shop takes a lot out of me, but that's always been true. It's just the normal, physical kind of tired, you know? I really hope I'm not confusing things by saying that. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate picture I can. I'm still getting up and doing what I need to do every day. \n\nDoes that make sense? Like, is it okay that my energy levels are mostly fine even though I'm... well, you know, struggling so much with everything else? I hope I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping through the night or do you wake up much earlier than you intend to?\n\nPatient: Actually, no, I've been sleeping okay. I feel almost bad saying no again, I really know you're trying to figure all this out and I want to be as helpful as possible. But usually, once my head hits the pillow, I'm pretty much out for the night. \n\nHauling sheet metal around all day kind of guarantees that I'm ready for bed by the time I get there, I guess. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, at least I'm getting my rest, right? I just hope it doesn't make it harder for you to figure out what's wrong with me since I don't have that problem. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, definitely not. I hope you don't think I'm... you know, that bad off. I mean, I know I'm struggling right now, but I would never do anything like that. \n\nI really value my life, and I know I have a lot to be thankful for, even when things are hard. I just... I want to make sure you know I'm safe, and I'm not, like, a danger to myself or anything. I'm just having a hard time dealing with everything lately. Is it okay that I don't have those kinds of thoughts? I just really want to give you the right answers so you can help me get back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been down most of the day, nearly every day, since your daughter left?\n\nPatient: Um, no... not most of the day, really. I mean, I definitely miss her, obviously, but I try really hard to keep a good attitude. I don't want to be a burden on anyone, you know? Especially at the shop, I just put my head down and try to stay upbeat. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm not taking this seriously, because I really am having a hard time with her being gone, but... I wouldn't say my mood is down *all* day, every day. I'm really doing my best to hold it together and put on a brave face. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not leading you down the wrong path.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in almost everything you do, even things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still enjoy things. I still like going to work, and... well, I try to keep busy. I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping all the time. \n\nI still try to watch my favorite shows, and I like talking to my friends when I see them. I mean, maybe things aren't *quite* as fun as they were before she left, but I definitely haven't lost interest in everything. I'm really trying to stay positive and keep up with my normal life. I hope that's what you're looking for? I just want to be clear that I'm trying my hardest not to let this completely take over.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have periods most days where your mood feels noticeably low, even if it's not constant all day long?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, obviously I have moments where I miss her, but I wouldn't say my mood gets noticeably low. I really try not to let it get to that point. \n\nIf I feel myself getting a little quiet, I just... I find something to do, or I focus harder on my work at the shop. I don't want you to think I'm just moping around on the floor or anything like that. I usually just try to shake it off right away and get back to work. Is it okay that I handle it like that? I just really want to do whatever I'm supposed to do to stay strong right now, and I hope I'm not doing it wrong.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there are times during the day when your mood feels more down or heavy, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really heavy. I mean, I guess I have moments where it hits me that she's far away, but I wouldn't call it heavy. I try really hard to catch myself before it gets like that. \n\nI just... I'll go organize some tools or check on a work order, just to keep my mind moving. I really don't want to be one of those people who just drags everyone down with them, you know? I guess I haven't really tried them enough lately to know for sure. I feel bad even saying that, because I know I should be keeping up with my hobbies. It's just... I haven't really had the energy to get the boat ready, or to set up my torch at home. \n\nSo it's not that I don't like doing those things anymore! I'm sure if I just forced myself to go out there, I'd probably still enjoy it. I just... I haven't been doing them. I hope that's an okay answer... I'm sorry if I'm not being very helpful. Do you think I should try to force myself to do them this weekend?\n\nDoctor: When you think about your metal sculptures or going out on the water, do you feel any sense of enjoyment or satisfaction just from thinking about doing them, even if you haven’t actually done them lately?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still like the idea of them, I think. Like, when I picture being out on the water, it sounds nice... in theory. The thought of it just makes me feel really tired instead of excited. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm really not trying to be negative about it. I know I need to get back to my normal life. I've even been snapping at the younger guys on the crew. That is *not* who I am, I swear. I try to be a good coworker. \n\nSo when it comes to the boat or my sculptures... I just don't see the point in them right now when I can barely handle my actual responsibilities. I hope you don't think I'm a terrible person for letting things slip this much at work and at home. Does that make me sound crazy? I just really hope it's normal to feel this off track when things change.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're a failure or that you're letting people down more than you should be, especially because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't really think of myself as a failure, exactly. I know I'm going through a hard time, and I'm trying my best to handle it. I really don't want to let anyone down, especially at work, which is why I feel so bad about snapping at the guys. \n\nBut I don't sit around thinking I'm worthless or anything like that. I know I'm a good mom and a hard worker, usually. I just... I feel like I should be handling this transition better, you know? Like I should be stronger. Is it normal to feel like you're letting yourself down a little bit when you can't just bounce back from something? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right idea about where my head is at.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the things you used to really enjoy—like your metal sculptures or being out on the water—does doing them sound appealing to you now, or does it feel like you wouldn't get any real satisfaction from them even if you tried?\n\nPatient: I... I think I already kind of answered this, but I don't mind explaining it again if I wasn't clear before. I really want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. \n\nLike I said, it just feels like they don't matter right now. I don't think I would get any real satisfaction out of them if I tried. It's just so hard to care about hobbies when I'm struggling so much with the important things, like my job and just... keeping my head above water since my daughter left. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know I'm repeating myself, and I really hope I don't sound like a broken record or like I'm just whining. I'm just trying to be as honest as I can so you can help me. Did that answer the question better this time?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’re not getting pleasure or satisfaction out of most things you do these days, even the things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: I... gosh, I feel like I'm just giving you the same answer over and over, and I really don't mean to be difficult. I just want to make sure I'm answering exactly what you're asking. \n\nI guess... yeah, it's pretty much most things right now. Even the things I used to love. It just all feels kind of flat. I'm really sorry, I know that probably sounds so negative, and I promise I'm usually a much more upbeat person. I just can't seem to find the joy in much of anything since she left. Is it okay that I'm feeling that way? Like, does that make sense for what I'm going through?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or have you felt restless or unable to sit still lately?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think I've had that. I hope it's okay to say no to that one, I really don't want to mess up your evaluation or anything. But physically, I feel like I'm moving at my normal pace. \n\nI'm still keeping up with all the heavy lifting and the regular pace at the shop, and I haven't felt jittery or like I can't sit still. I'm just... you know, moving normally, maybe just a little tired. I hope that's a good sign? Like, maybe that means I'm not doing too terribly? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information, so I hope it's alright that I don't really have that issue.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or that you're feeling more tired out than you used to, even when you're not doing anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: I mean... I know I said just a second ago that I was a little tired, but... no, I don't think I have less energy overall. Like, I'm not exhausted when I'm just sitting around at home or anything. \n\nMy job at the shop takes a lot out of me, but that's always been true. It's just the normal, physical kind of tired, you know? I really hope I'm not confusing things by saying that. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate picture I can. I'm still getting up and doing what I need to do every day. \n\nDoes that make sense? Like, is it okay that my energy levels are mostly fine even though I'm... well, you know, struggling so much with everything else? I hope I'm answering these right for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping through the night or do you wake up much earlier than you intend to?\n\nPatient: Actually, no, I've been sleeping okay. I feel almost bad saying no again, I really know you're trying to figure all this out and I want to be as helpful as possible. But usually, once my head hits the pillow, I'm pretty much out for the night. \n\nHauling sheet metal around all day kind of guarantees that I'm ready for bed by the time I get there, I guess. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, at least I'm getting my rest, right? I just hope it doesn't make it harder for you to figure out what's wrong with me since I don't have that problem. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no. No, definitely not. I hope you don't think I'm... you know, that bad off. I mean, I know I'm struggling right now, but I would never do anything like that. \n\nI really value my life, and I know I have a lot to be thankful for, even when things are hard. I just... I want to make sure you know I'm safe, and I'm not, like, a danger to myself or anything. I'm just having a hard time dealing with everything lately. Is it okay that I don't have those kinds of thoughts? I just really want to give you the right answers so you can help me get back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been down most of the day, nearly every day, since your daughter left?\n\nPatient: Um, no... not most of the day, really. I mean, I definitely miss her, obviously, but I try really hard to keep a good attitude. I don't want to be a burden on anyone, you know? Especially at the shop, I just put my head down and try to stay upbeat. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm not taking this seriously, because I really am having a hard time with her being gone, but... I wouldn't say my mood is down *all* day, every day. I'm really doing my best to hold it together and put on a brave face. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so I'm not leading you down the wrong path.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in almost everything you do, even things you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I still enjoy things. I still like going to work, and... well, I try to keep busy. I mean, maybe things aren't *quite* as fun as they were before she left, but I definitely haven't lost interest in everything. I'm really trying to stay positive and keep up with my normal life. I hope that's what you're looking for? I just want to be clear that I'm trying my hardest not to let this completely take over.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you have periods most days where your mood feels noticeably low, even if it's not constant all day long?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I don't think so? I mean, obviously I have moments where I miss her, but I wouldn't say my mood gets noticeably low. I just really want to do whatever I'm supposed to do to stay strong right now, and I hope I'm not doing it wrong.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there are times during the day when your mood feels more down or heavy, even if it's not all day long?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really heavy. Does that sound okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that there are parts of most days where your mood feels down or heavy, even if it gets better at times?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say it feels heavy.
